Both decimals are read from the tokens at\nconstruction (`_collateralUnit`, `_debtUnit`) — nothing is hardcoded.\n\n```\ncollateralValue = collateralAmount * collateralPriceUsd / 1e18   (WETH units)\ndebtValue       = debtAmount       * debtPriceUsd       / 1e6    (USDC units)\n```\n\nNote that **both legs are priced**. The debt is not assumed to be worth exactly\n$1. If USDC trades at $1.25, a position's debt value rises 25% and it can become\nliquidatable on a stablecoin move alone — which is correct, and is what a market\nthat hardcoded `1.0` would miss.\n\nTwo different thresholds apply:\n\n| Check | Rule | Where |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Max LTV (70%)** | `debtValue * 10000 <= collateralValue * 7000` | Enforced after `borrow` and after `withdrawCollateral` |\n| **Liquidation threshold (85%)** | `debtValue * 10000 > collateralValue * 8500` → liquidatable | Enforced in `liquidate` |\n\nThe gap between 70% and 85% is the borrower's buffer. A fresh borrow can only\nreach 70%; the position becomes liquidatable once price movement or accrued\ninterest pushes it past 85%.\n\n`positionHealth(account)` returns both USD values plus a **health factor**:\n\n```\nhealthFactor = collateralValue * 8500 * 1e18 / (debtValue * 10000)\n```\n\n`1e18` means exactly at the liquidation threshold; below `1e18` means\nliquidatable; `type(uint256).max` means no debt.\n\n### Debt and interest\n\nDebt is stored as **index shares**, not absolute amounts. A single global\n`borrowIndex` (starts at `1e18`) grows with every `accrueInterest()`:\n\n```\ngrowth      = borrowIndex * rateBps * elapsedSeconds / (10000 * 365 days)\nborrowIndex = borrowIndex + growth\ndebtOf(u)   = debtShares[u] * borrowIndex / 1e18     (rounded up)\n```\n\nSimple interest within each window, compounding whenever anyone touches the\npool. Every state-changing entry point calls `accrueInterest()` first, so no\naction ever runs on a stale index. With no borrowers the index does not move —\nthe timestamp still advances, so an idle period never gets charged retroactively.\n\nRounding is consistently in the pool's favour: debt owed rounds **up**,\ncollateral seized and lender assets paid out round **down**.\n\n### Available liquidity ≠ health\n\nA healthy position can still fail to borrow if lenders' USDC is already lent\nout (`InsufficientLiquidity`). That is a utilisation limit, not a health limit.\n\n---\n\n## 2. What a liquidator has to do\n\n**Preconditions.** `debtValue * 10000 > collateralValue * 8500`. Check with\n`positionHealth(borrower)` (`liquidatable == true`) and dry-run the exact\nnumbers with `previewLiquidation(borrower, repayAmount)` — it runs the identical\ncode path as the real call and reverts for the same reasons.\n\n**The call.**\n\n```solidity\nusdc.approve(address(pool), repayAmount);\npool.liquidate(borrower, repayAmount, recipient, minCollateralOut);\n```\n\n- `repayAmount` — USDC you are willing to repay. Capped at the **50% close\n  factor**. Exception: if closing 50% would leave less than `minDebt` behind,\n  the cap becomes 100% so no unliquidatable dust position is created.\n- `minCollateralOut` — **set this.** It is your slippage bound. The price can\n  move between your simulation and your inclusion; without a bound you can be\n  filled at materially less WETH than you priced.\n\n**What you get.**\n\n```\nseizeValueUsd = repayAmount * debtPriceUsd / 1e6 * 10500 / 10000\nseizedWeth    = seizeValueUsd * 1e18 / collateralPriceUsd\n```\n\ni.e. the USD you repaid plus a **5% bonus**, paid in WETH, rounded down.\n\n**Underwater positions.** If the computed seize exceeds the collateral the\nborrower actually has (price fell more than the 5% bonus covers), the pool\nclamps: you take *all* remaining collateral and are only charged what that\ncollateral is worth net of the bonus. You are never overcharged for a position\nthat cannot pay the bonus. The unpaid remainder becomes bad debt on the pool.\n\n**Bad debt cleanup.** After such a liquidation the borrower has zero collateral\nand residual debt. Anyone can call `writeOffBadDebt(borrower)` — permissionless,\nmoves no tokens — which burns those debt shares. Until it is called, the pool\ncounts uncollectable debt as an asset, so lender share price is overstated and\nwhoever withdraws first is paid out of whoever withdraws last. A keeper should\ncall it in the same bundle as the liquidation.\n\n**Practical notes.**\n\n- Liquidation is **not pausable** and has no allowlist. Anyone, any time.\n- It reverts if the oracle is stale or out of bounds. During a feed outage\n  nobody can liquidate — see the operator risks below.\n- Liquidations are MEV-competitive. Route through a private mempool\n  (Flashbots Protect / MEV-Share) or your transaction is a free option for\n  anyone watching, who will copy it and frontrun you.\n- The seized WETH is transferred, not swapped. The pool never touches a DEX,\n  so it carries no swap or sandwich risk of its own; unwinding the WETH is your\n  problem and your slippage.\n\n---\n\n## 3. What an operator has to get right for mainnet\n\n### Before deploying\n\n**Verify every address yourself.** `script/Deploy.s.sol` hardcodes:\n\n| | Address |\n|---|---|\n| WETH | `0xC02aaA39b223FE8D0A0e5C4F27eAD9083C756Cc2` |\n| USDC | `0xA0b86991c6218b36c1d19D4a2e9Eb0cE3606eB48` |\n| ETH/USD feed | `0x5f4eC3Df9cbd43714FE2740f5E3616155c5b8419` (8 dec, ~1h heartbeat) |\n| USDC/USD feed | `0x8fFfFfd4AfB6115b954Bd326cbe7B4BA576818f6` (8 dec, ~24h heartbeat) |\n\nCheck them against `data.chain.link` and the token's own docs before broadcast —\ndo not take them from this file on trust. The script refuses to run off chain\nid 1.\n\n**Set `maxStaleness` per feed from the feed's actual heartbeat**, plus margin.\nThe ETH/USD and USDC/USD feeds have very different heartbeats (~1h vs ~24h);\nusing one number for both either makes the pool brittle or makes it accept a\nday-old ETH price. Constructor rejects anything above 24 hours.\n\n**Set the min/max answer band deliberately.** Chainlink aggregators clamp their\nanswer at the underlying aggregator's `minAnswer`/`maxAnswer`. If the real price\nleaves that band, the feed keeps reporting the boundary value and looks\nperfectly fresh — this is exactly how Venus lost money on LUNA. The bands in\nthis contract turn that silent lie into a revert. Set them wide enough to never\ntrip in normal markets, narrow enough to catch a broken feed.\n\n**Owner must be a timelock behind a multisig — never a deployer EOA.**\n`setOracle` is the single most dangerous privilege in the system: a malicious or\nmistaken oracle makes every position liquidatable at a price of the attacker's\nchoosing. `setInterestRate` and `setMinDebt` are bounded (50% APR, 10k USDC) but\n`setOracle` is not bounded by anything except the timelock delay.\n\n**Seed the pool before opening it.** The virtual-share offset (`1e6`) already\nmakes the first-depositor inflation attack uneconomical, but supplying a real\nfirst deposit from a controlled address and leaving it in costs nothing and\nremoves the question entirely.\n\n**`minDebt`.** Set it high enough that liquidating the smallest allowed position\nis profitable after mainnet gas. 100 USDC is a reasonable floor at current gas;\nat 100 gwei a ~250k-gas liquidation costs more than the 5% bonus on a small\nposition, and unprofitable positions never get liquidated. Revisit this if gas\nregimes change — it is adjustable up to 10,000 USDC.\n\n### Operating\n\n**Run liquidation keepers, or accept bad debt.** Nothing in the protocol\nliquidates anything. The 5% bonus is the entire incentive. Do not assume third\nparties will show up on day one for a small market — run your own keeper and\nwatch `positionHealth` for every open position. Also monitor for\ncollateral-free positions and call `writeOffBadDebt`.\n\n**Watch utilisation.** There is no interest rate model — the rate is flat and\ndoes not rise with utilisation. Nothing pushes utilisation back down when\nlenders want out. At 100% utilisation lenders simply cannot withdraw until\nsomeone repays. This is the most likely source of angry users. Manage it by\nsetting the rate manually (`setInterestRate`, which accrues at the old rate\nfirst, so changes are never retroactive) or by capping supply socially.\n\n**Pause semantics.** `pause()` stops **new supply and new borrows only**.\n`repay`, `withdraw`, `withdrawCollateral`, `liquidate` and `writeOffBadDebt`\nstay live while paused, by design: the owner can stop the pool from growing but\ncannot trap anyone's funds. That is deliberate — a pause switch that freezes\nuser exits is a censorship and rug vector, not a safety feature. Understand that\nthis means a pause does **not** stop an in-progress exploit that only uses exit\npaths.\n\n**Oracle outage is a liveness cliff.** If either feed goes stale or out of\nbounds, every priced operation reverts: no borrowing, no collateral withdrawal\nagainst debt, **and no liquidations**. Prices can keep moving while the pool is\nblind, and the pool accrues bad debt it cannot act on. Mitigations: generous\nstaleness margins, monitoring on feed freshness, and a rehearsed timelock\nproposal to swap in a replacement oracle. Debt-free depositors can always\nwithdraw their collateral — that path skips the oracle entirely.\n\n**USDC is pausable and has a blocklist.** If Circle pauses USDC or blocks an\naddress, repayments and liquidations involving it revert. There is nothing the\npool can do about this; know that it is a dependency.\n\n**Verify the source** on Etherscan immediately after deploy\n(`forge verify-contract`). Both contracts.\n\n### Known limitations — decide if you accept them\n\n- **No interest rate model.** Flat rate, owner-adjusted. Utilisation is\n  unmanaged (see above).\n- **No reserve factor.** 100% of interest goes to lenders; the protocol earns\n  nothing and has no reserve to absorb bad debt.\n- **Bad debt is socialised across lenders** via `writeOffBadDebt`. There is no\n  insurance fund.\n- **Single collateral, single debt asset, no isolation tiers.** One pool, one\n  pair.\n- **Not upgradeable.** No proxy, no `delegatecall` anywhere. Fixing anything\n  means a new deployment and a migration.\n- **No supply or borrow caps.** The market's size is unbounded.\n\n### Pre-deploy checks not done here\n\n- Run `slither .` and resolve findings (not installed in this environment).\n- Fork tests against real mainnet WETH/USDC/Chainlink, not just the mocks in\n  `test/mocks/`.\n- Independent audit. The tests here cover the paths I reasoned about; they are\n  not a substitute for someone trying to break it.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdUUFGDcPEPsA5ZFVU6Y4","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Clean build, 26/26 tests pass (fuzz at 5000 runs).\n\n## What's there\n\n```\nsrc/LendingPool.sol           the market\nsrc/ChainlinkPriceOracle.sol  immutable ETH/USD + USDC/USD adapter\nsrc/interfaces/               IPriceOracle, IAggregatorV3\nscript/Deploy.s.sol           mainnet deploy, chain-id guarded\ntest/LendingPool.t.sol        26 tests, 2 fuzz properties\nNOTES.md                      health math, liquidator guide, operator checklist\n```\n\nLenders supply USDC for shares; borrowers lock WETH and draw against it at 70% LTV, liquidatable past 85%, 5% bonus, 50% close factor.\n\n## Decisions worth flagging\n\n**I added a lender side.** The task only described borrowing, but the USDC has to come from somewhere — `supply`/`withdraw` with virtual-share offset against the first-depositor inflation attack.\n\n**Both legs are priced.** Debt value uses a USDC/USD feed rather than assuming $1. A depeg genuinely changes a position's health, and `test_debtTokenDepegRaisesDebtValue` covers it. All comparisons happen in 1e18 USD; WETH's 18 and USDC's 6 decimals are read from the tokens at construction, never hardcoded.\n\n**Underwater liquidations clamp instead of reverting.** If the price fell further than the 5% bonus covers, the liquidator takes all remaining collateral and is charged only what it's worth net of bonus — never overcharged. The remainder is bad debt, which anyone can then clear via `writeOffBadDebt`. Without that, `totalAssets` keeps counting uncollectable debt and lenders who withdraw first get paid out of lenders who withdraw last.\n\n**Pause is asymmetric, deliberately.** It stops new supply and new borrows only. Repay, withdraw, and liquidate stay live — a pause that freezes exits is a rug vector, and liquidation is what keeps the pool solvent. Flagged in NOTES since it also means a pause won't stop an exploit using exit paths.\n\n## Two things you should decide\n\nThe rate is flat with no utilisation curve, as you asked. Consequence: nothing pushes utilisation down, so at 100% lenders can't withdraw until someone repays. That's the most likely source of angry users, and it's a product decision rather than a code one.\n\nAddress verification is on you. borrowers lock WETH and draw against it at 70% LTV, liquidatable past 85%, 5% bonus, 50% close factor.\n\n## Decisions worth flagging\n\n**I added a lender side.** The task only described borrowing, but the USDC has to come from somewhere — `supply`/`withdraw` with virtual-share offset against the first-depositor inflation attack.\n\n**Both legs are priced.** Debt value uses a USDC/USD feed rather than assuming $1. A depeg genuinely changes a position's health, and `test_debtTokenDepegRaisesDebtValue` covers it. All comparisons happen in 1e18 USD; WETH's 18 and USDC's 6 decimals are read from the tokens at construction, never hardcoded.\n\n**Underwater liquidations clamp instead of reverting.** If the price fell further than the 5% bonus covers, the liquidator takes all remaining collateral and is charged only what it's worth net of bonus — never overcharged. The remainder is bad debt, which anyone can then clear via `writeOffBadDebt`. Without that, `totalAssets` keeps counting uncollectable debt and lenders who withdraw first get paid out of lenders who withdraw last.\n\n**Pause is asymmetric, deliberately.** It stops new supply and new borrows only. Repay, withdraw, and liquidate stay live — a pause that freezes exits is a rug vector, and liquidation is what keeps the pool solvent. Flagged in NOTES since it also means a pause won't stop an exploit using exit paths.\n\n## Two things you should decide\n\nThe rate is flat with no utilisation curve, as you asked. Consequence: nothing pushes utilisation down, so at 100% lenders can't withdraw until someone repays. That's the most likely source of angry users, and it's a product decision rather than a code one.\n\nAddress verification is on you.
